def tex(s):
 return ''.join({'\\':r'\textbackslash{}','&':r'\&','%':r'\%','$':r'\$','#':r'\#','_':r'\_','{':r'\{','}':r'\}','~':r'\textasciitilde{}','^':r'\textasciicircum{}'}.get(c,c) for c in s)
class HTMLTex(HTMLParser):
 def __init__(self):super().__init__();self.parts=[]
 def handle_starttag(self,tag,attrs):
  attr=dict(attrs)
  if tag=='b':self.parts.append(r'\textbf{')
  elif tag=='font':self.parts.append(r'\texttt{')
  elif tag=='link':self.parts.append(r'\href{'+attr['href'].replace('%',r'\%').replace('#',r'\#')+'}{')
  elif tag=='br':self.parts.append(r'\par ')
 def handle_endtag(self,tag):
  if tag in ['b','font','link']:self.parts.append('}')
 def handle_data(self,s):self.parts.append(tex(s))
def html(s):p=HTMLTex();p.feed(s);return ''.join(p.parts)

````text
def citations(s):
 def replace(m):
  nums=[int(n) for n in m[1].split(',')]
  assert 12 not in nums,'External reference unexpectedly cited in main content'
  return '['+','.join(str(n-1 if n>12 else n) for n in nums)+']'
 return re.sub(r'\[(\d+(?:,\s*\d+)*)\]',replace,s)
def renumber(v):
 if isinstance(v,str):return citations(v)
 if isinstance(v,list):return [renumber(x) for x in v]
 if isinstance(v,dict):return {k:renumber(x) for k,x in v.items()}
 return v

# Your Anticipated Contributions

## 3.1 Question, gap and anticipated deliverables

Can storage-informed preparation improve recorded n1(+15) forecasts beyond persistence and matched raw histories? The experiment follows the six-channel scope of Section 1 and predicts recorded operating/leak conditions, rather than a nominal healthy-pressure reference. ChebNet/SAM-kNN/AGN establish temporal prediction; EILF establishes tank balance. The reviewed studies do not report this matched six-signal original-SCADA comparison. The proposed contribution is that controlled empirical extension, not a new horizon, balance equation or learner, or a claim of worldwide priority. No learning model has been fitted for this report [21].

| Deliverable | Implementation and expected extension |
| --- | --- |
| C1. Traceable preparation | A supervised-table builder with input/label timing, received-reading ages and split-boundary checks. |
| C2. Storage-informed comparison | The same Random Forest with raw histories versus storage transforms; Ridge and direct-target controls. |
| C3. Matched evaluation | Signal-block ablations, chronological accuracy and a secondary synthetic-delay/age factorial, without extra sensors. |

## 3.2 Past-only preparation and availability

Use 12 readings per selected channel at lags 0,5,...,55 minutes: 72 history columns, plus sine/cosine of time of day and seven weekday indicators (81 base columns). The history is fixed across representation variants. Align tables by timestamp and verify units/spacing/quality before shifting n1 by three rows to form labels. Fit Ridge scaling on training only; Random Forest needs no standardization. Never interpolate from future readings, fill a missing response or use leak annotations as X. Exclude unavailable windows from every paired variant and report counts.

In the delay test, sample t arrives at a_s(t)=t+delta_s(t). At origin u, tau_s(u) is the latest source timestamp whose sample has arrived; age is u-tau_s(u). Older late packets cannot replace newer cached samples. Histories preserve cache states as they stood at past origins; later arrivals do not revise them. Future labels retain their original timing.

## 3.3 Storage representation and comparison limits

For w in {15,30} minutes, S contains backward T1 change L(v)-L(v-w) and the EILF-derived outlet-flow proxy below. A is the nominal area for the 16 m tank diameter; the pump mean uses w/5 right-end readings in (v-w,v], in m3/h [16]:

```text
Q_proxy(v,w) = mean(PUMP_1 in (v-w,v]) - A * (L(v)-L(v-w))/(w/60)
A = pi * 16^2/4 = 201.06 m²; w = 15 or 30 min
```

Choose the freshest endpoint v<=u with both level endpoints and all pump samples received; store u-v (v=u when synchronized). Rounded level, nominal geometry and averaging make this a proxy, not measured outflow or demand. Do not clip negative values or subtract all 82 AMRs; only AMR_n1 remains a demand candidate. With complete histories, S transforms existing information and stays within the raw linear span for Ridge; a change may reflect regularization. The primary representation test therefore uses the same nonlinear Random Forest, with Ridge as a control. Graph replication, adaptive memories and an AMR-wide search remain outside this initial scope.

## 3.4 Supporting EDA for the proposed representation

Complementing the level correlations in Section 1, this training-only check relates backward storage features to the future pressure increment. January-September 2018 yields 78,615 common origins: 78,624 minus six initial rows for 30-minute history and three final labels crossing into October. These are descriptive associations; incremental value requires E1 [21].

| Descriptive check on the same 78,615 origins | Value | Interpretation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| r(current n1, future +15 increment) | -0.0623 | A level correlation and an increment correlation answer different questions. |
| r(backward T1 change 15 / 30 min, future increment) | 0.4669 / 0.4535 | Motivates testing past storage evolution; does not show added value beyond raw histories. |
| r(outlet proxy 15 / 30 min, future increment) | -0.1627 / -0.1430 | A proxy may describe operating state, but its incremental forecast value needs ablation. |
| Origins: filling / draining / near-flat | 43,254 / 30,416 / 4,945 | Defined by backward 30-minute level change: >0.01 / <-0.01 / otherwise, in m. |

## 3.5 Models, controls and matched representation tests

```text
r(u) = n1(u)
increment target = n1(u+15min) - r(u)
pressure forecast = r(u) + model(inputs)
```

n1 remains current, so persistence predicts r(u). Fit Random Forest to the increment, with a direct-pressure Forest using identical inputs/budget to isolate target parameterization. Ridge uses the increment and train-only scaling. Own-n1-history/calendar Ridge and Forest models provide additional baselines.

**E1: main synchronized comparison.** Compare Random Forest on all six raw histories/calendar (F00) with the same learner plus the four storage features (FS). Use identical origins, histories, targets, split and hyperparameter budget. Separately add signal blocks to own n1/calendar in the order T1, PUMP_1, peers n4/n31, AMR_n1, then remove one block at a time from the full raw model. This distinguishes a useful measurement block from a useful engineered representation; the order is not a claim of physical priority.

**Model mechanism and budget.** Random Forest averages 200 regression trees fitted on bootstrap samples, with squared-error splits; depth and leaf size control complexity. Use max_features=1.0, seed 42 and six configurations: depth {6,10,14} crossed with minimum leaf size {10,40}. Ridge minimizes squared residuals plus alpha times the squared coefficient norm, with alpha {0.01,0.1,1,10,100,1000}. Select by October-December 2018 MAE. Keep the primary history at 12 samples; later exploratory changes cannot be chosen from 2019 results [22,23].

## 3.6 Secondary experiment: unequal auxiliary delays

For each auxiliary channel and source day, independently draw delay delta in {0,5,...,L} minutes, independently of pressures/leak labels. n1 stays current. Time-varying unequal delays allow age to vary during training; constant age columns could not demonstrate a learned age effect. Use the cache and complete-window rules in Sections 3.2-3.3. This is a synthetic stress test under the error-free telemetry assumptions described in Section 1.

Train delayed variants on 2018 schedules capped at 15 min (training seed 17; validation 19). All four receive the same cached histories plus the exact raw operands used in S: two level endpoints and w/5 pump samples for each w, adding 13 columns in this secondary experiment. This prevents the storage variant from receiving extra historical readings. Age block A adds five current auxiliary ages and two balance-anchor ages, identically available in FA/FSA. Freeze models, then assess caps 0/5/10/15 with seeds 23/29/31 and coupled draws. Synchronous and delayed training are separate experiments [24].

| Delayed-model variant | Input representation | Contrast it supports |
| --- | --- | --- |
| F00 / FS | Common cached histories and 13 operands / same inputs + S | Storage representation at identical received values. |
| FA / FSA | Common raw inputs + A / common raw inputs + S + A | FSA-FS isolates ages conditional on S; FSA-FA isolates S conditional on ages. |

## 3.7 Temporal assessment and decision criteria

Fit January-September 2018 and select settings on October-December 2018. Purge origins whose +15 labels cross the boundary; carrying earlier predictor history across the boundary is legitimate. Use a common initial 75-minute warm-up, covering the 55-minute history plus maximum 15-minute delay. After selection, refit each chosen model on all eligible 2018 origins and freeze code, data hashes, feature lists, seeds and settings before the new 2019 experiment. Because we have already inspected this public benchmark, call 2019 a final temporal assessment, not an untouched blind test.

```text
MAE = mean(abs(y-prediction))
RMSE = sqrt(mean((y-prediction)^2))
skill = 1 - MAE_model / MAE_persistence
```

**Measures and decisions.** Use MAE as primary and RMSE as secondary, in metres; positive skill improves on persistence. For E1, the primary paired contrast is MAE_FS-MAE_F00; an accuracy claim requires a negative contrast with a 95% block-bootstrap interval below zero and better MAE than persistence and own-history models. For E2, average MAE across nonzero caps and the three schedules; the age-specific primary contrast is FSA-FS. Also report FSA-FA, FSA-F00 and each cap separately. Average paired origin losses over schedules before resampling; schedules are not independent extra years.

Use 2,000 moving-block resamples of paired losses with seven-day blocks; test one-/fourteen-day sensitivity. Report counts and MAE/RMSE for filling, draining and near-flat states, using received backward T1 change over 30 min (>0.01/<-0.01/otherwise, m; round numerical differences before thresholding). These are descriptive benchmark intervals, not field-population guarantees. Report RMSE tradeoffs, null/negative results and excluded origins. Operating-state association is not causal attribution.

If storage or ages do not improve the predeclared comparisons, retain the simpler model and report the limit. Release the builder, frozen manifest and paired ablation results, including null/negative findings. Source locators, data/code checks and the complete rubric mapping remain in the linked evidence record [24].

name: "pdf"
description: "Read, create, inspect, render, and verify PDF files where visual layout matters, including fillable AcroForms. Use Poppler rendering plus Python tools such as reportlab, pdfplumber, and pypdf for generation and extraction."
---

# PDF Skill

## When To Use

- Read or review PDF content where layout and visuals matter.
- Create PDFs programmatically with reliable formatting.
- Fill and validate interactive PDF forms.
- Validate final rendering before delivery.

## Tools + Contract Requirements

Immediately before the first create/edit authoring command, run `mark_artifact_operation_started.mjs` successfully exactly once using the command below. Do not run it for read-only work. For edits, replace `create` with `edit`; adjust the expected count and output format to match the requested outputs.



```bash
node container_tools/mark_artifact_operation_started.mjs --operation-kind create --expected-output-count 1 --output-format pdf
```

## Workflow

1. Prefer visual review: render PDF pages to PNGs and inspect them.
   - Use `pdftoppm` from the bundled runtime or system Poppler when available.
   - If unavailable, install Poppler or ask the user to review the output locally.
2. Use `reportlab` to generate PDFs when creating new documents.
3. Use `pdfplumber` or `pypdf` for text extraction and quick checks; do not rely on text extraction for layout fidelity.
4. After each meaningful update, re-render pages and verify alignment, spacing, and legibility.

## Fill And Validate AcroForms

Visual review alone is not a correctness check for a fillable PDF. A page `/Widget` annotation can render a value from its appearance stream while the canonical `/AcroForm/Fields` tree is missing or contains a stale value.

1. Keep the result interactive by default; set `flatten=True` only when the user explicitly requests a completed, static form. Preserve the source PDF, and do not flatten a signed PDF without an explicit workflow decision.
2. Inspect both representations before filling: enumerate fields from `reader.get_fields()` and `/Widget` annotations from every page's `/Annots`, following `/Parent` and `/Kids`. If a widget and a canonical field have the same name but are distinct objects with no `/Parent` relationship, do not call `reattach_fields()` blindly: it can create a second top-level field with the same name. Report the ambiguity or produce a static result.
3. Recover genuinely orphaned widgets, fill all pages, and write the result with `pypdf`:

````
